市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6
ISM服務業指數分項指標變動對比表ISM服務業PMI主要分項指數變化

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6
長端殖利率維持今年以來高檔
資料來源：兆豐銀行整理， 2026.10.6

• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8
月貿易逆差擴大13.7%至1,056億美元，且超出市場預期的1,021億美元。經通膨調整後，8月商品貿易逆差擴大至1,147億美
元，同樣為2025年3月以來最大。
• 觀察美國本土投資能否進一步轉化為出口能力：AI建設帶動半導體與設備進口，加上能源成本上升及關稅政策干擾，推升進
口額至歷史新高。不過，強勁內需也顯示消費與企業投資仍具支撐力。隨著第三季財報季即將展開，根據Factset數據，分析
師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

• 實質薪資持續成長，內需復甦動能增強：日本8月實質現金收入年增1.5%，連續第8個月成長，名目工資與基本工資均年增
3.8%，顯示薪資成長已逐步抵銷通膨對購買力的侵蝕；剔除獎金、加班費及抽樣偏差後，全職勞工薪資仍成長2.8%，反映
薪資上漲具有一定持續性。實質所得改善有助家庭消費與內需回升，進一步支撐企業營收與獲利展望。
• 薪資與通膨並行，日股中長期仍具配置價值：薪資持續上升有助日本形成薪資增加、進而消費擴大，導向企業營收改善的正
向循環，同時也強化日本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化的條件。雖然升息可能推升日圓與資金成本，但金融股可受惠於利差
擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

中國已備案的LLM數量

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。
資料來源：華金證券，2026.10.5

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

2026 年供過增長率最低的一年：Alphaliner 預估 2026 需求成長 2.5%， 供給成長
率 4.7%，2026 年船舶供給成長率是近幾年最低的，目前貨櫃船在手訂單1400 萬
TEU，未來三年面臨新船大量下水壓力，然而公司認為受到地緣政治影響船期不穩
定性高、碼頭使用率偏高易受突發事件導致塞港、2028 年美西港口工人合約到期
等均影響有效運力，即使紅海復航採逐班逐航線，影響也會被抵銷，因此2027 年
的名目供給能否 100%轉為實質運力有待考驗 。 目前全球運力因為碼頭作業能量受
限、氣候因素、繞道、降速等影響 11%運力。 
美國零售進口 2026 年預計成長 1%：根據美國零售協會預估，2026 年 1-7 月貨
櫃進口 14.97 百萬 TEU(+0.3%YoY) ， 9 月進口量達到高峰 2.31 百萬
TEU(+10%YoY)，10 月開始下滑，但仍保持年成長，2026 年預估進口 25.7 百萬
TEU(+1%YoY)。

2026 OCP Global Summit –
MGX Upgrades Take Shape;
Maia 300 on Display
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Howard Kao

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
At the rack level, Maia 300 is the key incremental story, while
Nvidia/AMD exhibits offered limited new system-level hardware.
Nvidia’s MGX component upgrades are the main supply-chain
watch points, although PTFE and next-gen interconnect and
cooling connector (ZQD) adoption remains uncertain.

Nvidia/AMD –  focus on ramps rather than new platforms. Most ODM displays
were similar to Computex Taipei (early June), so timing of ramp is key. For Nvidia,
VR200 racks have started a small-volume ramp, with the Nvidia Groq 3 LPX rack
following in late 2026/early 2027 and standalone Vera CPU racks in late 1Q27/early
2Q27, based on our checks. For AMD, Helios remained largely unchanged with
recent checks, with racks ramping in 1Q27, with pilot-build observations pointing to

Shennan Circuits for EAM PCBs and Fositek/Parker for quick disconnects.
Maia 300 -  a larger in-house inference opportunity. Microsoft plans production
deployments in 2027 as it scales Maia toward multi-gigawatt deployments. Each
rack integrates 96 accelerators within 167 kW. Based on that, each gigawatt of
deployment would imply 5 to 5.5K racks, and we believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could
be the ODM partners, while our pilot-build observations point to ISU Petasys for the

accelerator PCB, AVC for fans, and Fositek/Staubli for quick disconnects.
PTFE - technical upside, but no confirmed timing of adoption. Nvidia is evaluating
PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon following the Kyber delay and showcased its
NVLink PTFE PCB with seven suppliers (though we think there are suppliers missing
from this list). Better signal performance and reduced reliance on Q glass are
potential benefits, but processing complexity, higher costs and yield/qualification

risks remain hurdles to commercial adoption.
Ultra Dense Interconnect - next-generation interconnect opportunity for FIT.
Nvidia showcased six suppliers for this next-generation interconnect, which is
intended to replace Amphenol’s current HD2 connector in midplane and cable
cartridge applications. However, we believe adoption is still about two years away. In
the meantime, the supply chain is working to improve yields and bring ASPs below
US$100, with pogo-pin supply currently a bottleneck. Our checks identify FIT as the

key design partner for Kyber’s planned board-to-board connector, a position that
bodes well for its supply share once the product ramps.
ZQD - higher flow density and an expected ASP premium. Nvidia’s new ZQD
targets higher flow rates per unit area and was displayed with 14 suppliers.
Although the supply chain is working to improve yields and lower costs, greater
design complexity should keep ASPs above current QD pricing of US$20–30.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 19, 2026 01:16 AM GMT

Major Takeaways from OCP Summit 2026
ODM/OEM: Almost all the ODM/OEMs had a booth at OCP, but during our visit, we feel
most of the products were largely similar to what was showcased at Computex, such as
the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, the LPX tray, the Vera standalone tray. So net, we think from a
hardware perspective, there was nothing new on Nvidia or AMD. We believe the Nvidia
VR200 rack is already starting ramp in small volume, with the LPX rack starting to ramp

later this year or early next year, and the Vera standalone CPU rack ramping late 1Q27 or
early 2Q27.
AMD: Wiwynn, Meta, and AMD each showcased the Helios rack, which was unchanged
from last year’s OCP display with one exception: this year’s version featured the actual
compute tray in place of last year’s dummy tray, allowing a closer look. That said, the
compute tray itself was not new to us, as we had already seen it at OCP Taiwan in August.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Shennan Circuits is one

of the pilot build PCB suppliers for the MI455 Helios EAM PCB.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek (MQD02) and
Parker (UQD04) are one of the pilot build quick disconnect connector suppliers for
the MI455 Helios compute tray.
• HPE displayed the Helios switch tray at the OCP Summit. We believe the other
supplier of the Helios switch trays is Celestica.
Microsoft: Maia 300 represents a major expansion of Microsoft’s in-house inference

infrastructure. Announced on October 12, 2026, with production deployments planned for
2027, it forms part of Microsoft’s commitment to scale the Maia family to multi-gigawatt
deployments and make it the primary inference platform for its products and services. The
design targets long-context reasoning, conversational AI, and multi-step agentic
workloads, with an emphasis on improving inference economics through coordinated
silicon, software, and system design.

Maia 300 rack integrates 96 accelerators within a 167 kW power envelope, providing
approximately 27.6 TB of aggregate HBM capacity. There are 4 accelerators per tray,
implying 24 compute trays per rack, including six high-radix switch ASICs that are
integrated into three liquid-cooled switch trays, providing the rack-local connectivity
required by all 96 accelerators. Microsoft projects 40% more tokens per megawatt than
leading GPU-based systems and up to 7× Maia 200’s rack-level token throughput, though

this takes into account that the Maia 300 rack  includes twice as many accelerators per
rack.
• The Official Microsoft Blog A two-tier Ethernet scale-up network connects up to
128 racks, or 12,288 accelerators, while liquid cooling removes more than 80% of
rack heat. Each compute tray includes 8x fans  (Exhibit 3 ), and based on our
observations at the OCP Summit, we believe AVC is one of the pilot build fan
suppliers for the sample compute tray.

• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe ISU Petasys is one of
the pilot build PCB suppliers for Microsoft’s Maia 300 AI accelerator.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek and Staubli are
pilot build MQD03 (quick disconnect connector) suppliers for the compute tray.

• We believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could be ODM partners for these racks.
• With each rack being 167 kW of power, one gigawatt of deployment would imply 5-
5.5K racks, assuming a PUE of 1.1-1.2.

Nvidia MGX Ecosystem
PTFE: We previously wrote that our supply chain checks suggest that Nvidia had initially
planned to adopt PTFE CCL for the very high-layer-count midplane in its Kyber
architecture. With Kyber now delayed, and Rubin Ultra no longer expected to use that
architecture, as previously discussed, the focus has shifted back to the Oberon
architecture. Nvidia is again evaluating PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon, and at this year’s

OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE PCB, with a total of 7 suppliers, including
VGT, TTM, Taiflex, SYTECH, Shennan Circuits, Kinwong, and Flexium (Exhibit 4  & Exhibit
5 ). However, our checks indicate that actual adoption for Rubin Ultra remains uncertain.
However, we find the supplier list puzzling. Our checks suggest that WUS PCB and
Unimicron are also working on this solution, and that Flexium, which has collaborated
with other PCB suppliers, may handle only a few process steps. Our checks also indicate

that Shennan Circuits, WUS PCB and VGT are now the leading PCB makers for this
solution, with relatively higher yields.
Advantages of PTFE
• Best signal performance: the lowest Dk and Df of the mainstream options, below
even standard M9-class CCLs. This matters most where insertion-loss budgets are
tightest, i.e. the highest speeds and highest layer counts.
• Stable electricals: the low Dk/Df hold up across frequency and temperature, and
PTFE absorbs very little moisture, so performance is stable in use.

• Less dependence on Q glass: because PTFE’s intrinsic dielectric constant is already
very low, reaching the target Dk requires less (and in some hybrid constructions,
none) of the expensive, supply-constrained Q glass. That is both a cost lever and a
supply-security benefit.
Disadvantages of PTFE

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
• Mechanically soft: low modulus makes it difficult to handle and to hold to
tolerance.
• Difficult lamination: PTFE does not flow like a thermoset, so bonding needs either
very high temperatures or lower-melting fluoropolymer (such as PFA
(polyfluoroalkoxy))/thermoset bond layers; its high, non-linear CTE stresses
copper adhesion and complicates layer-to-layer registration, and pressing typically
requires vacuum or an inert atmosphere with longer cycles.

• Difficult drilling: the soft, gummy resin tends to smear and can tear, and any
ceramic filler is abrasive on tooling. Also, the hole wall will not accept electroless
copper without an added activation step (sodium etch or plasma) on top of normal
desmear.
• Higher cost and qualification risk: materials and fabrication are both more
expensive, the qualified supply base is narrower, and yields at very high layer
counts carry risk.
Exhibit 4: Nvidia's NVLink PTFE Board

Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.

### Page 6

M
Idea
6
Exhibit 5: Nvidia PTFE PCB Supplier List
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
Ultra Dense Interconnect: Currently, Nvidia's NVL72 racks are using Amphenol's Paladin
HD2 connectors for VR200 NVL72 midplanes and cable cartridges. Our previous checks
done around Computex 2026 in June, indicate ASP could reach US$70-90+ per set. For
the next-generation Kyber racks, our checks have previously indicated that a new midplane
connector called "Voronoi" will be introduced, and FIT is the key design partner for this

new board-to-board connector. At this year’s OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased a new
connector it calls the “Ultra Dense Interconnect”, with six suppliers in total. This was our
first in-person look at the design. We believe adoption is still two years out, and the
supply chain is currently focused on improving yields and optimizing costs to bring the
ASP below US$100. Our checks suggest that pogo pin supply is currently a bottleneck for
this connector.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Exhibit 6: Nvidia next-generation Ultra Dense Interconnect
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
ZQD: At COMPUTEX in Taipei earlier this year, we noted that our checks indicated Nvidia
was planning to introduce a new quick disconnect (QD) design for next-generation server
racks to achieve higher flow rates per unit area as cooling requirements rise. At this year’s
OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased that design, called ZQD, with 14 suppliers in total. Our

checks indicate the supply chain is also working to improve yields and optimize costs to
bring ZQD ASPs down. However, given the greater complexity of the design, we expect
ZQD ASPs to remain above current QD pricing of US$20–30 per set.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部對拓凱未來營運看法轉趨正向 ，主要是考量：(1)占營收比
重最大的自行車業務在 2H26 出現改善，隨著主力客戶新品銷售表
現較佳，帶動訂單增加，預期 2027 年自行車業務有望止跌回溫。
(2)航太、安全帽及其他類業務接單保持強勁，其中航太受惠於航
空座椅需求增加，安全帽則有高階產品及新客戶訂單貢獻，加上精
密塑件業務持續擴張 ， 將有助於優化產品組合 。(3) 2Q26 受子公司
一次性費用及匯率等因素干擾，毛利率與獲利下滑，但隨下半年出
貨規模擴大、產品組合改善，預期 2H26 獲利能力將逐步回升。研
究部預估拓凱 2026 年 EPS 13.52 元(-9%YOY)，2027 年EPS 15.28
元(+13%YOY)。因公司營運重回成長軌道，且產品組合改善有利於
中長期獲利表現，故以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益
比，目標價 183 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要：

1. 主力客戶新車種上市，自行車業務回溫 ： 
拓凱營收約有 50% 來自碳纖維自行車車架與組件，最大核心
客戶為全球知名自行車 品牌-Specialized。雖然公司的自行
車業務在1H26衰退近二成，但受惠主力客戶 年中新品上市並
在終端市場銷售亮眼 ， 客戶對拓凱積極追加車架 、 輪圈與前叉
等複材組件訂單 ， 帶動拓凱近期自行車業務顯著改善 。 在去年
下半年低基期與今年新車拉貨潮帶動下，預計 2026全年自行
車營收減幅將收斂至個位數（研究部估減少8%YoY）。2027年
隨客戶新品需求與庫存去化完畢，預 估自行車業務營收將能
回溫，成長3%YoY。

2. 安全帽、航太及其他類業務展望正向，成長動能強勁： 
自行車以外業務為拓凱營運成長的核心動能 。 其中 ， 拓凱的安
全帽業務近年出貨增加 ， 且產品結構大幅改善 ， 高階產品占比
已達2/3，預計帶動今年安全帽營收大幅增長30%YoY，且因引
進電子智能安全帽新客戶，預期明年該業務營收仍可成長
7%YoY。球拍業務方面，今年出貨暢旺，預期今年球拍營收能
創下歷史新高 ， 全年度球拍營收預計成長18%YoY。 不過 ， 因近
兩年基期拉高，明年看法較謹慎，預計小幅衰退5%YoY。航太
醫療業務方面 ， 航太接單展望樂觀 ， 預期今年該類別營收增長
14%YoY，且1Q27起新增飛機座椅訂單將正式投產 ， 預計明年該
類別營收將再有12%YoY的增速 。 另外 ， 歸類在其他類之子公司

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

新鴻洲部分，受惠自行車煞把手、電子TOF精密塑件等產品出
貨增加 ， 營運動能依舊偏強 ， 預計該業務2026年、2027年營收
分別成長23%、13%YoY。

3. 一次性負面因素淡化，3Q26毛利率與獲利將回升： 
回顧拓凱2Q26財報 ， 受提列子公司一次性費用的衝擊 ， 導致單
季毛利率下滑至31.3%(季減3個百分點，年減6.7個百分點)，
加上業外有逾6千萬元的匯損，拖累季度獲利表現，2Q26稅後
淨利2.67億元(-19%QoQ，-5%YoY)，2Q26EPS下滑至2.94元。然
而 ， 此費用屬於非經常性的短期會計調整 ， 本業高階複材競爭
力並未受損 。 下半年隨自行車業務改善 ， 拓凱3Q26合併營收回
升至27.16億元(+6%QoQ，+12%YoY)。 此外 ， 由於一次性因素淡
化 ， 加上高階安全帽 、 航太及其他類產品出貨增加 ， 產品組合
優化，研究部預估拓凱3Q26毛利率將回升至33.8%，營業利益
5.41億元(+37%QoQ，+13%YoY)，稅後淨利3.55億元(+33%QoQ，
+1%YoY)，3Q26EPS 3.91元，獲利將較第2季明顯改善。

4. 2026年營收近百億元，預估2026年EPS 13.52元： 
受惠於安全帽 、 球拍 、 航太及其他業務成長 ， 抵銷自行車業務
的衰退 ， 預期拓凱全年營收可望接近百億元 ， 研究部預估拓凱
2026年合併營收99.73億元(+8%YoY)。不過，由於2Q26受一次
性費用及匯率等因素干擾，今年度毛利率預估降至33.1%，營
業利益16.91億元(-2%YoY)，稅後淨利12.28億元(-9%YoY)，
EPS 13.52元。

5. 各業務成長帶動獲利回升，預估2027年EPS 15.28元： 
展望2027年 ， 隨自行車業務恢復成長 ， 航太及安全帽新訂單持
續貢獻，以及其他類業務穩健擴張，研究部預估 2027年營收
104.34億元(+5%YoY)， 毛利率回升至33.8%， 營業利益18.50億
元(+9%YoY)，稅後淨利13.88億元(+13%YoY)，EPS 15.28元。

6. 越南產能持續擴充，並積極佈局新應用商機 ： 
因應客戶分散生產基地與供應鏈調整需求，拓凱持續擴充越
南複合材料產品線 ， 目前除自行車前叉 、 車架等零組件外 ， 亦
逐步增加航空座椅零組件的生產，未來航太板材及球拍等產
品亦有機會導入越南製造 。 公司也已著手規劃越南二期廠房 ，
強化海外生產彈性 。 此外 ， 中科后里新廠除作為營運總部及研
發據點外 ， 也將支援新業務開發 。 初期鎖定人形機器人關鍵的
腰部與膝蓋複材結構件與客戶進行共同開發，同時也積極切
入農業與商用無人機 、 低軌衛星等先端應用 。 子公司新鴻洲方

面 ， 則運用精密射出及模具技術 ， 計畫開拓半導體與人形機器
人零件等應用 。 雖然新事業短期營收貢獻仍有限 ， 但隨產品開
發與客戶認證推進，長期有助於拓展複合材料及精密零組件
的應用領域。

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。  
群益預估耕興 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 12.44 元 /13.73 元： 群
益預估耕興 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 49.35 億元 /53.89 億元，
YoY+9.80%/+9.19% ；稅後純益 12.68 億元 /13.99 億元，
YoY+15.58%/+10.35% ；稅後 EPS 12.44 元 /13.73 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

個股報告

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
미국 록빌·5공장 가세로 3분기 기대치 부합 전망 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 3분기 연결 매출액은 1조 3,637억 원(+8.4% Y oY), 
영업이익은 6,092억 원(-3.8% YoY, 영업이익률 44.7%)으로 시장 기대치에 부합할 
전망이다. 5월 파업에 따른 생산 차질에도 1~4공장(총 60.4만 리터)의 풀가동과 
신규 공장의 매출 기여가 외형 성장을 뒷받침할 것으로 예상한다. 미국 록빌 
공장(6만 리터)은 3분기부터 매출 기여가 본격화되고, 5공장(18만 리터)도 
인증용(PPQ) 배치 생산을 통해 매출이 확대될 전망이다. 2분기에는 미국 공장·5공장 
관련 비용이 선반영됐으나, 3분기에는 매출 증가에 따른 고정비 부담 완화로 
영업이익률이 전분기 대비 0.3%p 상승할 것으로 예상한다. 
항체 중심 CDMO에서 펩타이드로 확장 
삼성바이오로직스의 2026년 신규 수주는 약 1.2조 원으로 전년 수주액의 약 20% 
수준에 그치고 있으나, 연말부터 내년까지 수주 모멘텀은 점차 강화될 것으로 
판단한다. 2025년 하반기 이후 미국의 의약품 관세 불확실성이 완화되며 글로벌 
제약사와의 논의가 늘어나고 있어, 신규 수주 계약으로 이어질 것으로 기대된다. 
11월 유상증자 납입이 마무리되면 6공장(18만 리터) 착공과 스위스 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 본격화되며 중장기 성장 기대감도 높아질 전망이다. 특히 폴리펩타이드 
인수가 연내 완료되면 2027년부터 연결 실적에 반영되는 동시에, 항체 의약품 
중심의 CDMO 사업을 GLP-1 등 펩타이드로 확장하는 계기가 될 전망이다. 
목표주가 200만 원으로 하향, 수주 회복이 관건 
삼성바이오로직스의 목표주가를 209만 원에서 200만 원으로 하향한다. 유상증자 
신주 발행에 따른 주당가치 4.7% 희석 효과를 반영했다. 폴리펩타이드 인수 효과는 
거래완료 이후 실적 추정치에 반영할 예정이다. 노조 파업 불확실성이 주가에 
부담으로 작용할 수 있어 신규 수주 모멘텀의 회복이 필요한 시점이다. 
목표주가 (하향) 2,000,000원  
현재가 (10/6) 1,310,000원

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일 
해당 사항 없음

## Document 236: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

## Document 254: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 278: EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 12 October 2026 (2026-10-12).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-12

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROPEAN UNION
Stronger trade posture pays off. The EU and China have agreed to
restrain hybrid cars exports to the EU to a level half or less than cur -
rently projected over the next four years, cutting exports by “several
million units” though still allowing them to rise. China also agreed to

drop its tariffs on EUR 4 bn of EU exports and provide easier access to
Chinese rare earths and magnets under a fast-track licensing sche -
me. These announcements were made at the conclusion of EU Trade
Commissioner Maroš Šefcovic’s visit to Beijing, following a recent es -
calation in trade tensions. Meanwhile, France and Germany released
a joint non-paper on Strengthening European resilience, addressing
global macroeconomic imbalances and market distorting practices

calling notably for:
i) Broadening the tools the European Commission may use to address
unfair trade practices (including undervalued exchange rates) or im -
port surges;
ii) Procedural changes to make them easier to use (qualified majority
of member states needed to block rather than authorise them). These
measures will be discussed during the next EU council on October
15-16th.
The European Commission also unveiled a new critical materials pac -

kage involving 46 strategic extraction, processing or recycling projects
spread across 16 member states (including 5 in France). The goal is to
reduce the EU’s dependence on China for critical metals.
EUROZONE
Price pressures are broadening, activity holds up. Producer price in -
flation accelerated further in August (8.3% y/y 1 from 5.8%), driven by
energy. Pressures are becoming more widespread, notably in capital
goods and above all intermediate goods (6.8%). They are partly offset

by non-durable consumer goods, which remain in deflation (-0.8%).
Given the PPI's close correlation with the HICP , this points to further
upside risks to consumer inflation. On the activity front, final September
PMIs confirmed the flash estimates, with the composite index still solid
at 53.1 and the manufacturing index stable at 52.7. The construction
PMI improved but remains depressed (43.4; +0.4 pt). Retail sales edged
up 0.1% m/m2 in August.

France
Modest spread relief as budget positioning starts. The spread between
French and German 10Y yields narrowed to 136 bps 3 on October 9
(mostly thanks to a decline in the French yield). As the Finance Com -
mittee of the National Assembly started discussing the 2027 budget,
the government reiterated its strong intent not to deviate from the
5% of GDP deficit target and to get a budget adopted in time, likely
without a vote. This is the most likely outcome — but not to be im -

plemented before 10 December (given mandatory timelines) — which
effectively removes any incentive for MPs to back unpopular measures
during the process. We can therefore expect heated debates (including
multiple no-confidence votes being tabled but rejected) and a lack in
fiscal discipline. In parallel, the "Rassemblement National" party ("Na-
tional Rally" in English) presented its fiscal strategy, which includes an
alternative budget for 2027 and an aggressive fiscal consolidation path

throughout 2032. The party called for a referendum to constitutionalise
1 Year-on-year
2 Month-on-month
3 Basis points
a “golden rule” that would require a deficit at least 0.5 pp below the
debt-stabilising threshold (roughly 3% of GDP), as long as the public
debt ratio exceeds 60% of GDP . The golden rule would require a budget
balance adjustment of 0.5 pp per year if the fiscal deficit is more than
1 pp above the stabilising threshold. The adjustment measures put

forward lack specifics but likely overstate savings, understate costs
and rely on overly optimistic growth assumptions (despite large fiscal
consolidation, at least 1.7% annually over the next three years — a
pace reached only 4 times in France since 2008, apart from post-pan-
demic reopening, and well in excess of the roughly 1% growth potential
estimated by most international institutions). That said, it is significant
that this party has chosen to fly the flag for fiscal discipline loud and

clear. Other parties should do the same.
Activity is holding up. New vehicle registrations rose 11% y/y in Sep -
tember, confirming a marked acceleration over the past three months
(+9.4% y/y in Q3). In Q3, growth came entirely from electric vehicles
(+112% y/y), whose share of registrations reached an all-time high
(38% in Q3, 41% in September). Export growth continued in August,
both in services (+5.2% y/y YTD) and in goods (+5.6% y/y YTD) — though

driven by prices rather than volume in the latter case. Manufacturing
output posted only a moderate rebound in August (+0.3% m/m; fol -
lowing cumulative declines of 2.8% over the previous three months),
limited by renewed supply constraints in Q3 (which limited produc -
tion at 31.5% of companies; +5 pp compared to H1). Industrial produc-
tion fell (-0.3% m/m) as a result of a decline in electricity production
(-2.4% m/m) driven by the heatwave. Credit growth remains dynamic.

Bank and market financing to NFCs saw steady growth in August (+4%).
New housing loans rebounded slightly in August (EUR 12.2 bn) fol -
lowing a relatively weak July, whilst interest rates continued to rise.
Consumer credit remained buoyant.
Upcoming: Banque de France monthly Business Survey (Monday), Bu-
siness creations in September (Friday).
Germany
Bullish official growth forecast upgrade despite mixed data. The
German government raised its 2026 GDP growth forecast to 1.3%

— the fastest pace since 2017 — from a prior estimate of 0.5%, citing
stronger-than-expected exports and a surge in government spending.
The 2027 forecast was also raised to 1.1% from 0.9%. Industrial pro -
duction was stronger than expected in August (+2.3% y/y versus -1.7%
in July; the highest level since March 2025 and higher than antici -
pated), driven mainly by strong growth in construction output, prima -
rily in public works (+10.5%; to the highest level since November 2021).

By contrast, manufacturing output fell by -0.2% (after +3.3% in July)
due to a decline in transport equipment production (lowest level since
April 2022). Likewise, new factory orders hit their lowest level in one
year in August (-10.6% m/m). However, this decline is mainly due
to a base effect. Export growth remains solid: +4.4% YTD, despite a
-0.8% m/m decline in August. Exports to the Eurozone continue to rise
(+6.3% y/y YTD), while exports to the U.S. have returned to positive

territory (+1.1%) and those to China continue to decline (-12.9%). The
trade surplus reached EUR 19.5 bn in August (+4% y/y YTD, despite a
-9.8% m/m decline).

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.37 / 12 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
ECONEWS Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
UNITED STATES
Mild slowdown in services and more rate hikes signaled. The non-ma-
nufacturing ISM survey fell 0.5 pp4 to 54.9 in September due to a sharp
slowdown in the business activity component (down 5.2 pp to 56.5 pp,
still a robust expansion). Employment rose and supply pressures in -
tensified for the first time since June, while the price gauge reached

its highest level (74, up 1.4 pp) since 2022. Meanwhile, the minutes
of the September FOMC meeting confirmed the Fed’s hawkish outlook
and noted that the AI-buildout carried growing weight in the reading of
current inflation data and outlook. Governor Waller and St. Louis Fed
President Musalem mentioned the need for additional rate hikes while
remaining flexible on timing. The New York Fed survey of consumer in-
flation expectations showed them rising to 3.9%. We continue to expect

two more hikes, the next one coming in December.
Upcoming: September CPI (Wednesday), September PPI and Retail
Sales (Thursday), September Industrial Production (Friday).
JAPAN
Solid wage growth fails to lift household spending. Total wage growth
eased to 3.8% y/y in August (down 0.5 pp) as bonus payments stalled
(0% y/y, after 5.3% y/y in July). This allowed continued real wage growth
at 1.5% y/y, extending an uninterrupted run of positive readings in 2026

as administrative measures kept inflation in check. However, this did
not prevent real household spending from contracting in August for the
ninth consecutive month (-3.1% y/y).
EMERGING MARKETS
The IIF reports that nonresident portfolio flows to EMs turned negative
at -USD 26.3 bn in September, after USD 11.4 bn in August, the first net
outflow since June, as a hawkish Fed lifted US real yields and the USD.
Equity outflows reached -USD 19.2 bn, led by heavy foreign selling of

Korean equities, while debt flows turned negative at -USD 7.0 bn as
hard currency demand faded and EM credit spreads widened late in
the month. Further hikes from DM central banks will create additional
headwinds, but EM should remain resilient as long as global growth
persists.
ASIA
China
Yuan policy defence. Coinciding with the Beijing visit of the EU trade
commissioner, the People's Bank of China issued an unusually lengthy
formal statement rejecting claims that the yuan is undervalued and

defending its exchange-rate management by reiterating that it has a
‘floating exchange rate regime’ with reference to a basket of currencies.
It further argued that China's trade surpluses are driven by rising in -
dustrial competitiveness rather than currency under-devaluation and
that exchange-rate adjustments will not resolve structural global im -
balances. Nonetheless, it allowed the yuan to hit a four-year high vs.
the CFETS basket — bringing its year-to-date gain to 5.7%. The PBOC

set the USD/CNY's reference rate at 6.7330, the strongest level since
2023.
India
Monetary tightening. The RBI raised its repo rate by 25bp to 5.5%, its
first hike since February 2023, and shifted to a “calibrated tightening”
stance amidst rising inflationary pressures. The RBI introduced new
measures to stabilise the rupee, including direct dollar supplies to
state-owned oil companies and tighter restrictions on FX derivatives.
4 Percentage points

EUROPE, MIDDLE EAST, AFRICA
Central Europe
Cautious monetary policy stance amid mild inflation uptick. Poland’s
and Romania’s Central banks kept policy rates unchanged at 3.75% and
6.5%, respectively. Inflation accelerated in September, with a mild up-
tick in the Czech Republic and Hungary (respectively 2.5% and 1.6% y/y)
and a more entrenched increase in Poland (4% y/y). Inflation is now
above the official target in the Czech Rep. and Poland, but fuel price

caps in both countries should contain inflation dynamics.
Gulf
PMIs for September point for a firm but uneven non-oil recovery. Both
Saudi and UAE PMIs stood at 55.3, driven mostly by export orders in
the UAE and by domestic demand in Saudi Arabia. On the contrary,
Qatar’s PMI fell again (to 46.7), while Kuwait’s PMI also declined but
remained in expansionary zone (at 52.4).
Türkiye
Manufacturing production contracted by 1.6% m/m in August, the lar-

gest monthly decrease since the beginning of the year. On a y/y ba -
sis, it fell by 2.3%. Both domestic demand and exports seem to have
contributed negatively to this poor outcome. On a positive note, in
September, CPI inflation (1.9% m/m, 27.9% y/y) slowed more than the
market expected, and exports rebounded.
LATIN AMERICA
Inflationary pressures slightly up across the region. In Brazil, headline
CPI inflation accelerated to 4.6% y/y from 4.2% y/y in August (above the

central bank's 4.5% target), partly reflecting the reversal of a tempora-
ry electricity price discount in August. In Mexico, inflation rose to 3.5%
in September from 3.3% in August, while in Colombia it reached 6.3%,
slightly up from 6.2% in August. Inflation remained high in Chile but
was stable at 4.1%. In Peru, it was also slightly up (4.2% vs. 4.1%); the
central bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 4.25% for the thirteenth
consecutive month but maintained an upward bias amid rising inflation

and energy prices.
COMMODITIES
Energy: Limited relief. Continuation of skirmishes in the Gulf of Hormuz
and escalation of the conflict between the Houthis and a Saudi-led
coalition left oil prices fluctuating around $100/b for Brent. However,
the complex security situation has been driving the cost of shipping to
record high levels and the price of physical barrel to early April levels.
Late on Friday, President Trump announced an agreement with Russian

President Putin to release to global markets 4.8 million tonnes of die -
sel (roughly 8 days of European consumption), provided conditions in
refineries allow for it, of which 300,000 tonnes immediately. The White
House also announced a suspension of sanctions on Russia’s exports
of diesel until April 2027. Energy experts expect a de minimis price
impact.
European gas prices (TTF) rose by 11.5% over the week to 82 €/MWh.
EU gas inventories reached 73.6% of full capacities on October 11 th

(the official target is 80% at end October).
Food inflation rose sharply in September. The FAO’s Food Price Index
grew by 5.8% y/y, up from just 3% in August. This was driven by cereal
(+17% y/y) and sugar prices (+15%).

## Document 347: Open mouth operations (2026-10-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-10-24).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market 
capitalisation fairly stable, while the MSCI China 
Index has declined c.20% over the past one year

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely

## Document 390: Top of Mind - IPO Surge_ A Red Flag for Markets_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - IPO Surge_ A Red Flag for Markets_.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-07-28

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
The US IPO market has reopened in a big way in 2026, with issuance already at a
record high. But this activity has raised two key concerns for the equity market:
whether it’s flashing a la te-cycle warning sign and whether the market can
comfortably digest so much new supply. So, how worried should investors be? The
IPO Initiative’s Jay Ritter, Acadian’s Owen Lamont, and GS’ Ben Snider agree that
we’re not in an “IPO wave” (yet) but differ on ho w much of a red flag the surge in

issuance is signaling. Snider argues that the late-cycle warning signs of past IPO
booms aren’t evident today and digestion concerns are overdone. Ritter notes that
while high new issuance volume has historically predicted lower future returns, the
signal has been weak. But Lamont is more cautious, calling an IPO wave one of the
“four horsemen” of a bubble (especially alongside a surge in debt issuance), but
stresses that IPO waves don’t necessarily signal the market top. Key to watch: firs t

day IPO returns and the all-important AI outlook.
Ben Snider, Chief US Equity Strategist, Goldman Sachs
“I’m less worried [about the surge in new issuance] than the average investor… the late-cycle warning signs that have
historically accompanied IPO booms aren’t evident today… [and] the equity supply-demand balance this year looks better
than most investors intuit.”
Jay Ritter, Director of The IPO Initiative and Professor Emeritus, The University of Florida’s Warrington College

of Business
“High new issue volume is a predictor of low future market returns. But, like most indicators, this signal works only slightly
better than chance… So, I wouldn’t view an IPO boom on its own as a sign that a market downturn is imminent.”
Owen Lamont, Senior Vice President and Portfolio Manager, Acadian Asset Management
“Historically, issuance waves paired with capex waves… have generally resulted in disappointing returns for equity holders. So,
